# ep9 decisions log

- This log records decisions D1-D21 and the user statements behind them. Since 2026-10-20 it also records the decisions that the agent team made under the user's delegation (§5). The options, decisions and re-check rules live in [plan/11-decisions.md](plan/11-decisions.md).
- Hard architecture decisions H1-H37 are not logged here. They live as ADRs in [adr/](adr/README.md).
- Rule: append only. A changed decision gets a new dated entry; the old entry stays.
- Each entry names its source. Only the user's own words decide, or the agent team under the user's delegation of 2026-10-20 (§5). Text from tools or workflow scripts is marked as such.

## 1. Before the plan

The user fixed 8 items before the plan was written: product, full ECMAScript scope, speed priority, the tsrs question, GC preference, `unsafe` allowed, out-of-scope items and the `third_party/` dependency style. See [11-decisions §1](plan/11-decisions.md#1-fixed-by-the-user).

## 2. 2026-10-20: M0 starts

**User statement (verbatim):** "go, let's start with your ultracode"

**Reading.** "go" starts M0 on the plan defaults. So D1, D2, D7, D8, D12 and D14 take the default that 11-decisions states. The workflow task for this run gave that reading; the user did not name each D item.

| ID | Question | Decision = plan default, as 11-decisions states it | Plan deadline | Affects |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| D1 | Final parser path | **E**. Option E: "C now, local oxc patches (≤ 3), D only on a P-gate, tsrs out of process" (C = oxc pinned) | M0 wk3 | H1, H26; P1-P5; M0-M1 |
| D2 | v1 platforms | "macOS arm64, Linux x64/arm64, Windows x64, all tier-1 from week 1. `ep9 check` and `ep9c build --hints` are unix-only until SP-tsrs-win passes. Windows arm64 after v1. iOS arm64 only if D16 = yes" | M0 wk1 | H28, G10; A3 |
| D7 | Proper tail calls | "Accept the gap: 35 tests, 0.07% [R09]. `TailCall` opcode reserved; no Rust recursion. The H14 ADR (M0 wk3) shows that frame reuse fits the layout, AOT frames included" | M0 wk3 | H14; M5, M8 |
| D8 | Toolchain | "Stable 1.99.1. The nightly build is opt-in" | M0 wk1 | H15, H26 |
| D12 | Upstream contacts | "The user's oxc contact, if confirmed. No tsrs request (its port rule forbids it). The oracle uses the tsrs public API (≤ 2 local patches, est.) [R13]" | M0 wk1 | P1; A1h; RK5, RK38 |
| D14 | Worker heap model | "Isolate per thread" | M0 wk3 | H11, H22, H23 |

Notes:
- **D1 in the repo.** The root `Cargo.toml` pins 6 oxc crates at `=0.155.0` from crates.io (skeleton, 2026-10-20; corrected 2026-10-21, the first text said 7).
- **D12 is not complete.** The default says "the user's oxc contact, if confirmed". No contact is confirmed in this log. Until the user names one, the oxc track runs without a contact; local patches carry more weight and P1 may trip earlier (11-decisions).
- **D7 and D14** had deadline M0 wk3. They are now fixed at M0 start. A later change needs an ADR amendment of the rows they affect (D14: H11, H22, H23).

### D3: team

| Item | Value |
|---|---|
| Plan default | "6 seniors (lead/front end, GC, VM, JIT/platform, runtime, AOT) + agents. v1 at about 57-100 months" |
| Recorded on 2026-10-20 | AI agents do the work now, not the 6-senior team. Agents fill the plan roles |
| Source | The workflow task text for this run. It matches the user's "let's start with your ultracode", but the user did not state a team size. **The user should confirm.** |
| Effect on estimates | PM figures are "senior human person-months" (09-milestones). They stay as a size of work. Calendar months = PM ÷ FTE does not apply to an agent team, so the plan's calendar estimates (for example v1 in about 57-100 months) do not apply directly |
| Not in the D3 options | The plan offers 2-3, 5, 6, 9 or 13 seniors plus agents. An agent-only team is a new answer |

Open follow-ups from D3 (for the user; not decided here):
- Human review. The plan puts human ownership on contract crates (CODEOWNERS) and an adversarial review on every `unsafe` change ([10-quality §6](plan/10-quality.md#6-agent-workflow)). With an agent team, the user is the only human. Who reviews? See ADR process question P-4 in [adr/README.md](adr/README.md#4-ownership-codeowners-and-the-adr-lint).
- Re-plan point. The plan says to check each "(est.)" number at its first milestone review (09-milestones). M0 exit is the first point to compare agent throughput with the PM estimates.

## 4. D15 framing note (choose at M0 exit)

**What the user chooses.** Two things, at two times ([11-decisions D15](plan/11-decisions.md#32-d15-d21-new-for-dual-mode), [05-aot §1](plan/05-aot.md#1-short-answer)):
1. At M0 exit: the product claim for AOT.
2. At M7 exit: the v1 timing, if A4 trails M7.

| Option | Claim | What it means for the plan | Gate that backs it |
|---|---|---|---|
| (a) default now | AOT = startup and warm-up, jitless speed, predictable latency, no JIT compile CPU, possibly smaller total shipped size. The JIT owns general peak | Plan as written. "Predictable latency" is claimed only where the SHORT-RUN latency metric shows it | FV1 (A3; K-A3), FV2 (A4; K-A4P), G13 (A1), G15 report |
| (b) default as A5 = M10 | (a) + "beat V8 at peak on opt-in sound typed numeric class code" | As a gated research stage after M7: needs D17 = yes and a T3-class backend. As a v1 goal: A5 moves before M8 and competes with M7 for staff | FV3 (≥ 1.1x V8 full on ≥ 2 of 4 TYPED-KERNELS and ≥ 1.3x A4 hinted); K-A5pre, K-A5 |
| (c) rejected (N10) | "AOT with TS types beats V8 in general" | No measured system supports it. The architecture would chase a peak gain that guard-removal studies put at 2-8% | none |

| Timing | Meaning | Cost |
|---|---|---|
| (t1) default | v1 waits for the A4 gates | v1 slips 0-6 months (est.) if A4 trails M7 |
| (t2) | v1 ships JIT mode, A1 and the jitless top tier on M7 timing; A4 follows in v1.x | Optimized-AOT, hybrid mode and HINTED facts wait for v1.x |

**Evidence the user has at M0 exit** (no FV gate is measured by then; [11-decisions §4](plan/11-decisions.md#4-evidence-for-d15-d21)):
- No measured JS AOT system beats V8 on general code. Static Hermes typed native is 1.5-3.2x slower than V8 full (geomean about 2.1x) [R15]. Its gap is implementation debt, not a ceiling [R15][R17].
- scriptc ran nbody about 1.15x faster than V8 at peak, but it refuses eval and changes JS semantics [R10][R17].
- Graal PGO native-image beat its own JIT (0.89-0.91 vs 0.92 of C2), with a profile trained on the same benchmarks [R11][R17].
- Hello-world startup and RSS gaps are runtime bootstrap effects, not AOT effects [R17].
- New in M0: the SP-Unit result (unit blob loads, calls, unwinds, GC-scans and falls back on 4 targets).

**What depends on the answer** ([11-decisions §2](plan/11-decisions.md#2-how-the-decisions-depend-on-each-other)): D15 feeds D17 (sound dialect, M10, FV3, K-A5) and D18 (hint investment, A1h, A4, K-A4H).

**Data comes back later.** M7 exit: SP-TypedBound (K-A5pre) and A4 progress against T2 → D17 and D15 timing. M8 AOT ship review: K-A4J, K-A4P, K-A4H, the AOT-vs-own-JIT report, latency and the G15 report → D11, D15, D18. No AOT part leaves v1 until the user confirms there.

**Questions for the user at M0 exit:**
1. Keep claim (a) for v1? Or make (b) a v1 goal, with its staff cost against M7?
2. Confirm that claim (c) stays rejected.
3. Any early view on timing (t1) vs (t2)? The plan asks for it only at M7 exit.

## 5. 2026-10-20: decisions made by the agent team under the user's instruction

**User statement (verbatim, relayed by the workflow harness):** "Don't ask me questions. Deepresearch and descide yourself"

**Reading.** The user hands every open item to the agent team: D1-D21, the R18 plan questions, the open ADR questions of H06, H11, H35 and H36, and the governance items. The agents decide each item with evidence. They prefer the reversible option when two options are close, and they make nothing public. The owner reviews at will and can override any entry. The D3 wording ("AI agents led by an orchestrator, with the human owner reviewing at will") comes from the workflow task text, not from the user's words.

**How the rows were made.** Five agents (gc-barrier, math, tech-gaps, governance, d-sweep) proposed decisions. A critic agent challenged each one. The apply agent made the final call: a "change" whose evidence held when re-checked → the better decision; a "keep" → the decision as proposed; every critic point is in the last column. Scratch evidence paths are under the session scratchpad `work/decide/<agent>/` (session-local).

**What this resolves.** §2 "The user should confirm" (D3) and the D12 note: decided below. §3 "Each stays open": every row is decided below. §4 questions for M0 exit: claim (a) kept, (c) stays rejected, timing (t1) kept.

Kinds: **now** = final until its re-check point or an owner override. **rule** = a written rule picks the outcome from gate data at the named point; nobody is asked a question. Confidence: [high] / [medium] / [low].

## 7. 2026-10-21: H15 dispatch on x86-64 (SP-Dispatch reopen)

**Source.** The H15 agent of the M0 workflow, under the user's delegation of 2026-10-20 (§5). The round started from the user's message, relayed verbatim by the workflow harness: "What are u watching? CI is already finished and failed". Inputs: CI run [39458801246](https://github.com/Brooooooklyn/ep9/actions/runs/39458801246) (commit `ea31dcb`), the x86-64 diagnosis (branch `diag/dispatch-x64`, 3 runs) and the Windows cgoto.c fix. Data: [measurements/2026-10-21-sp-dispatch-x64.md](measurements/2026-10-21-sp-dispatch-x64.md). Decision record: [adr/H15-interpreter-dispatch.md](adr/H15-interpreter-dispatch.md).

**Trigger.** The SP-Dispatch gate failed on linux-x64: "sibling ≥ 1.2x faster than match (geomean): 0.812", and match beat sibling on every kernel. That is the DISPATCH-GATE reopen rule (§5.4). The plan says a failed spike gate reopens its ADR, so H15 reopened before its first draft.

|---|---|---|---|---|---|

Sibling beating match on a model is H15-Q1 input, not a trigger (the vendor split is known) | **Superseded 2026-10-21 by §10 H15-X64-T0.** The R06 gain of 1.3-1.5x holds on aarch64 only |

## 8. 2026-10-21: BytecodeGen v0 (F1)

**Source.** The bytecodegen agent of the M0 workflow, under the user's delegation of 2026-10-20 (§5). The workflow task text quotes the user: "Don't ask me questions. Deepresearch and descide yourself"; "Keep working on our plan" (workflow script text). Code: [crates/ep9_frontend/src/bytecodegen/](../crates/ep9_frontend/src/bytecodegen/mod.rs). The design table G1-G11 is at the top of `mod.rs`.

```text
source -> parse + semantic -> scope::analyze -> BytecodeGen (G1-G11) -> UnitBuilder::finish -> verify -> VerifiedUnit
```

| ID | Decision | Conf. | Evidence | Reopen rule | Note |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| BCG-API | Entry points `compile_script`, `compile_module`, `compile` return `VerifiedUnit` or `CodegenError` (Compile, Unsupported, Limit, Internal). `check_determinism` builds twice on two threads and compares bytes and content hash | high | `bytecodegen/tests.rs::entry_points` | The VM or the tools need another result type | M0 exit 12 helper |
| BCG-SCOPE (G1, G2) | The scope pass decides every binding location. BytecodeGen pushes one context for each scope with `needs_context`, in tree order. Temporaries use register marks (scope based, deterministic) | high | 87 golden files; context check (BCG-CTX-CHECK) | A register optimizer arrives (M2) | No re-allocation in codegen |
| BCG-SCRIPT-CTX (G3) | A script whose top level needs a context gets the script context descriptor as unit scope 1. Later ids move up by one. Scope 0 keeps the global declarations for `DeclareGlobals` | medium | `var-script-this`, `var-direct-eval` goldens | H13 global object design needs another shape | Keeps the verifier rule "outer scopes come first" |
| BCG-FINALLY (G5, G6) | `finally` uses a token register and a value register, then `SwitchOnSmi` (V8 model). `for-of` is a try-finally with a `done` register | high | `ctl-finally-*`, `iter-for-of-*` goldens | Generators (M2) need another shape | Handler tables stay static |
| BCG-TDZ (G8) | A hole store at scope entry only when some reference checks it. Globals and module cells: the VM checks | medium | `var-tdz*` goldens | A TDZ bug in test262 runs (M1) | V8 rule |
| BCG-RUNTIME (G9) | ABI 2: runtime ids 6-18 for global init, direct eval, classes, super, accessors, object spread and `__proto__`. The opcode table does not change | medium | `ep9_bytecode/tests/golden/abi-history.txt` row 2; H02 change log | H13 runtime table (M1) | Bumps `ABI_VERSION` to 2 |
| BCG-POS (G10) | Every instruction that can throw has a position entry (allocations excepted). New builder call `FunctionBuilder::set_throw_position` gives the default | high | 0 missing on 72,147 corpus units | M0 exit 7 needs columns of another kind | Small API addition to ep9_bytecode |
| BCG-UNSUPPORTED (G11) | An unsupported construct fails the whole unit with `CodegenError::Unsupported` and a position. v0 does not support: generators, async, `await`, `for await`, private names, class fields, static blocks, auto-accessors, decorators, `import()`, `import.meta`, `using`, `delete super`, direct eval with spread arguments | high | corpus classes (BCG-CORPUS) | Months 2-3 plan | No silent wrong code |
| BCG-ERR-BOX | Codegen functions return `Result<T, Box<CodegenError>>` | high | Debug stack per AST level of the `call` form: 8,512 B before, 3,776 B after; budget 5,830 B (`stack_tests.rs`) | The budget changes in `stack.rs` | Release worst 1,232 B (budget 3,270 B) |
| BCG-CTX-CHECK | A test-only data-flow check follows context values through each function and checks Create*Context parents, closure contexts, slot depth and slot range | medium | 4 mutation tests catch wrong units that the verifier accepts; 0 errors on 72,147 units | H02-Q2 moves the check into the verifier | Input for H02-Q2 |
| BCG-CORPUS | Corpus run: test262 + `third_party/bench-*`, 54,522 files, 104,375 compiles, each built twice: 0 internal errors, 0 panics, 0 determinism mismatches, 0 missing positions, 0 context errors. A corpus digest (SHA-256 over sorted path, mode and unit hash) compares hosts and targets: `decb0871...8fce76` over 72,147 units on macOS arm64 (debug and release), macOS x86-64 (Rosetta), Linux arm64 and Linux x86-64 (Docker) | high | `cargo test -p ep9_frontend --release --lib bytecodegen::corpus -- --ignored --nocapture` | — | Not the tools/test262 subcommand |
| BCG-SCOPE-ATTR | Three `#[cfg_attr(not(test), expect(dead_code, ...))]` attributes in `crates/ep9_frontend/src/scope/mod.rs` are removed. BytecodeGen now calls those functions, so `expect` fails the build | high | `cargo clippy -p ep9_frontend --all-targets -- -D warnings` | — | Outside the bytecodegen path; one-line change each |

**VM contracts.** BytecodeGen v0 assumes these. The VM agent must check them.

| Contract | Where it shows |
|---|---|
| `StaGlobal` and `SetNamedProperty` take strictness from the function flag | sloppy vs strict goldens |
| The VM checks TDZ and `const` for global lexical bindings, also in `LdaGlobalInsideTypeof` | `var-global-lexical` |
| Module cells start as the hole; imports are hole-checked by the VM | `module-*` goldens |
| Accessor runtime arguments that are `undefined` mean "absent" (`DefineAccessorInLiteral`, `DefineClassAccessor` merge with the other half) | `lit-object-methods`, `class-base` |
| New function-context slots and registers start as `undefined` | all goldens |
| Mapped `arguments` needs a parameter-to-slot map | `var-arguments` |
| `DefineClass(ctor, heritage)` sets the prototype, `constructor` and the home object of the constructor | `class-*` goldens |
| `ResolvePossiblyDirectEval` receives the unit scope id of the call site | `call-eval`, `var-direct-eval` |
| `DeclareGlobals` reads the declarations from unit scope 0 | every script golden |
| RegExp flag bit i = letter i of `dgimsuvy` | `lit-regexp` |

**Known gaps (open).** `with (o) f()` calls `f` with an `undefined` receiver. The default derived constructor and a spread call that is not last iterate an array (observable). Script completion values are not kept (f0 returns `undefined`). Module hoisted functions are set at f0 start, not at module instantiation (H02-Q3). The `super` base is read at access time.

## 9. 2026-10-21: PR #1 build decisions and doc sync

**Source.** The docs-sync agent of the M0 workflow, under the user's delegation of 2026-10-20 (§5). The agents that built PR #1 (branch `m0/weeks2-3`, commit `fdad2b4`) reported the choices in §9.1; no earlier section records them. Each row was checked in the code on 2026-10-21. The Evidence column names the place. All reported items were confirmed; none was dropped.

### 9.1 Decisions in the PR #1 code

| ID | Decision | Conf. | Evidence | Reopen rule | Note |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| T0-NOT-IMPL | An opcode or path that T0 v0 does not run throws a catchable `Error` with the message "not implemented in ep9 v0: <what>". Never a panic, never UB | high | `crates/ep9_vm/src/error.rs` (`not_implemented`); `src/interp/ops.rs` (module doc); test `tests/exceptions.rs::type_errors_and_not_implemented_are_catchable` | Each item goes when its feature lands | Every v0 shortcut in `ops.rs` is marked `// v0:` |
| T0-NATIVE-WORD | A native word on the VM stack (caller fp, return pc, FunctionInfo) is stored as `addr + 2^49`, a subnormal double pattern. So every slot in `[base, top)` is a valid `Value`, and the GC scans the VM stack as one precise slice | high | `crates/ep9_vm/src/frame.rs` (`native`, `native_addr`); `DOUBLE_ENCODE_OFFSET = 1 << 49` in `ep9_value` | A target with addresses at or above 2^49 (debug assert in `native`) | Decoding is one subtract. Object cells use the same encoding (`object.rs`) |
| T0-STACK-LIMIT | Both stack limits give a catchable `RangeError` "Maximum call stack size exceeded": the VM register stack cap (`push_frame` finds no room) and the native stack bound (`sp > native_limit`; `native_limit` = stack low end + clamp(stack size / 4, 128 KiB, 1 MiB)). The isolate stays usable after it | high | `crates/ep9_vm/src/call.rs`; `runtime.rs` (`native_stack_ok`); `isolate.rs` (`native_margin`); tests `vm_stack_overflow_is_a_catchable_range_error` and `native_stack_overflow_is_a_catchable_range_error`, every dispatch kind | The H23 ADR (limits) sets other values | The native check runs at JS entries, host calls and recursive runtime routines |
| T0-DEBUG-STACK | Debug builds only: the threaded handlers (sibling call, `become`) panic when the native stack pointer reaches 64 KiB below `native_limit` (`DEBUG_SLACK`). So the RangeError path keeps 64 KiB to unwind. The panic text says to use `Dispatch::Match` | high | `crates/ep9_vm/src/interp/dsl.rs` (`debug_native_stack_check`) | — | Debug builds have no tail calls, so sibling dispatch grows the native stack by one frame per instruction |
| T0-COLD | Match loop (the x86-64 release expansion, H15): each cold block is an out-of-line `#[cold]` `#[inline(never)]` function (`cold::*`). Each arm stays small, so LLVM can copy the dispatch into the arms | high | `crates/ep9_vm/src/interp/dsl.rs` (`expand_match!`); `cargo xtask disasm-check --match-loop` | The `--match-loop` check fails on T0 (H15-X64 reopen rule, §7) | **Corrected 2026-10-21 (§10 H15-X64-T0):** the match loop is the debug expansion; LLVM does not copy its dispatch on T0; the cold functions stay. The threaded expansions have one cold handler per cold block |
| T0-NEW-TARGET | v0 keeps new.target in the FEEDBACK header slot of a `[[Construct]]` frame (argc bit 30). `LdaNewTarget` reads it there. Host functions get it as an argument. The slot is free until H16 | medium | `crates/ep9_vm/src/call.rs` (`push_frame`); `src/interp/ops.rs` (`LdaNewTarget`) | H14-Q1 (open; M1: classes, `Reflect.construct`); H16 needs the slot | The H14 text was fixed the same day (clarify) |
| VM-CELL-KINDS | v0 cell kind ids: STRING 1, OBJECT 2, FUNCTION 3, ARRAY 4, ERROR 5, PROPS 6, ELEMENTS 7, CONTEXT 8, ITERATOR 9 (array and string iterator, 48 B). A FUNCTION cell has 56 B: object (32 B), FunctionInfo (native word), context, `[[HomeObject]]` | high | `crates/ep9_vm/src/object.rs` (kind table, `mod fun`, `mod iter`) | Shapes (H8, H17) and H19 strings replace the v0 object model (week 4) | Kind 0 is reserved (`ep9_heap::KindId`) |
| T262-INFRA | `ep9-test262 exec`: a panic, a timeout (soft per-run timeout, default 20 s; then a hard kill) or a worker crash is an infrastructure error, not a failure. Any infrastructure error makes the run exit 2 before it compares or writes the expectation file | high | `tools/test262/src/exec.rs` (module doc; `if infra.is_empty()` guards the compare and `--update-expectations`); usage text in `main.rs` | — | Same rule as `parse` |
| T262-NAME | The exec expectation file is `expectations/test262.<target-triple>.jit.txt` (NAMING `<suite>.<target>[.<mode>].txt`, mode `jit`). First file: `test262.aarch64-apple-darwin.jit.txt` | high | `tools/test262/src/main.rs`; [expectations/](../expectations/README.md) | NAMING reopen rule (§5.4) | |
| HEAP-LOS-VA | Each LOS cell takes a run of whole 256 KiB chunks of cage VA (at least one). It commits only up to the OS page after the cell. So the default 64 GiB cage holds at most 262,144 LOS cells (64 GiB / 256 KiB), fewer when small pages use chunks | medium | `crates/ep9_heap/src/heap.rs` (`alloc_large`); `memory.rs`; `DEFAULT_CAGE_SIZE` in `crates/ep9_platform/src/va.rs` | **Open.** Decide at the S1 design (M1) whether small LOS cells share chunks. Earlier if a test or workload hits the limit | Accepted for S0. A LOS cell is above 8 KiB (H09-Q3), so its VA cost is up to about 30 times its size |
| HEAP-SIZEFN | A `CellSize::Dynamic` size function may read only its own cell | high | `crates/ep9_heap/src/cell.rs` (`SizeFn` doc) | — | The heap calls it on cells found through a VO bit or a LOS header (`cell_size`) |
| HEAP-SCRATCH-LIFO | `ScratchRoots` scopes nest LIFO, checked at run time: a push into a scope that is not the innermost panics, and a scope dropped out of order panics (not while a panic unwinds) | high | `crates/ep9_heap/src/roots.rs`; test in `src/tests/gc.rs` (`not the innermost`) | — | 03-gc §2 `ScratchRoots` |

The root now pins 7 oxc crates | high | `cargo metadata --locked` and `cargo build -p ep9_frontend --locked` pass; `Cargo.lock` unchanged (sha256 `e88aee50...f94cd8b`) | The next oxc bump PR bumps all 7 together | Closes H01-Q1 (fill). Evidence: "user messages as quoted by the workflow task". Note: "Records the instruction that the §5.4 REMOTE-CI row asks for" | The user wrote both messages in the session (times in UTC). Evidence: the session transcript. The note adds the correction | The old text was wrong. The transcript shows the user's own messages (§9.3). The decision does not change: the repo stays private; no force-push to `main` |

The row was edited in place, as in §6. This table keeps the old text.

## 10. 2026-10-21: PR #1 review fixes, CI gates and H15 on T0

**Source.** The follow-up agent of the M0 workflow, under the user's delegation of 2026-10-20 (§5). The rows come from the PR #1 review fixes (commits `9d2a0fb` to `2111b19` on `m0/weeks2-3`, the base of `m0/followups`) and from two branches merged into `m0/followups`: `ci/m0-gates` (`b1b0925`) and `h15/t0-x64` (`7b92f7d`). Each row was checked on 2026-10-21 against the code, the commit messages, the review findings or the cited data. The Evidence column names the place.

### 10.1 PR #1 review fixes

**Review.** A Codex adversarial review ran on 4 slices of PR #1: bytecode (B), vm and test262 runner (V), heap (H) and frontend (Fr). The Codex service filter blocked the platform slice twice, so a Claude agent reviewed `ep9_platform` (P). Each finding came with a reproduction or with code evidence (Fr3: the emitted control flow). All 16 Codex findings were fixed: B1-B2, V1-V5, H1-H3, Fr1-Fr6. The platform review gave P1 and P2.

| ID | Decision | Conf. | Evidence | Reopen rule | Note |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| B1 | A handler target must be at or after its range end. A throw then only goes forward, and every loop inside a function passes a `JumpLoop` (an interrupt poll). Chosen over an interrupt poll on the unwind path: it keeps "only `JumpLoop` goes back" (H02 F6), adds no poll or deopt point and needs no VM change | high | `crates/ep9_bytecode/src/verify.rs` (guarantee table, "target before the range end"); test `verifier::handler_back_edge_does_not_load` (before: a `Throw` loop that `terminate()` did not stop); 72,147 corpus units still verify | A construct needs a handler that targets code before its range | No ABI bump: the format does not change, and ABI 1 and 2 never shipped. [H02](adr/H02-bytecode-abi.md) change log row |
| B2 | The verifier enforces the reader's rules: canonical strings, defined scope and function flag bits. So a unit from `UnitBuilder::finish` always loads back | high | `UnitString::is_canonical`, `ScopeFlags::is_defined`, `FunctionFlags::is_defined`, used by the verifier and the reader; test `verifier::builder_output_meets_the_reader_rules` | — | H02 change log row |
| V1 | A class constructor throws a TypeError on [[Call]] from Rust (`call_internal`: ToPrimitive, `HostCx::call`, `Isolate::call`), as the `Call*` slow path does (ECMA-262 10.2.1 step 2). Construct from Rust is unchanged | high | `crates/ep9_vm/src/call.rs`; test `calls::class_constructor_needs_new_on_rust_calls` (all dispatch kinds, with and without GC stress); e2e `classes/coerce_without_new.js` | — | Before: `{ valueOf: C } + 1` ran C's body |
| V2 | test262 exec: a run whose watchdog deadline passed is a timeout infrastructure error, even if it returns (pass, fail or harness error) | high | `tools/test262/src/exec.rs` (watchdog `expired`); test hook `EP9_TEST262_FAULT=late` | — | A native loop that does not poll termination can outlast the deadline and then return |
| V3 | An async test fails on any `Test262:AsyncTestFailure:` line, before or after a completion. It passes only on exactly one `Test262:AsyncTestComplete` line, so a second `$DONE()` is a failure | medium | `exec.rs` (async check); INTERPRETING.md (test262 `d53ebc8b`) says nothing about a second call | test262 states a rule for a second `$DONE()` | Product call: a duplicate callback is an engine bug worth seeing |
| V4 | A runtime negative test passes only if the thrown value's constructor name is the expected type. The message text does not count. `ep9_vm::Exception` gets one field, `constructor` (`v.constructor.name`, data properties only, runs no JS) | high | `exec.rs` (`exception_matches`); `crates/ep9_vm/src/isolate.rs`; test `negative_types_match_the_constructor` | — | Before: `throw "TypeError: x"` passed a TypeError test |
| V5 | `CodegenError::Internal` (a builder or verifier rejection) is an infrastructure error, for the test, a negative parse test and harness files. So `--update-expectations` can never list it | high | `exec.rs`; test hook `EP9_TEST262_FAULT=internal`. Full run unchanged: 9,767 / 53,616 pass, 0 infrastructure errors | — | |
| H1 | `alloc(kind, size)` checks the size of a Fixed kind before any path runs (TLAB, overflow TLAB, LOS) and returns `AllocError::WrongSize`. A release check, not a debug assertion | high | `crates/ep9_heap/src/heap.rs`; test `fixed_size_kind_rejects_other_sizes` | — | Before: a wrong size let the sweep zap a live neighbour's header |
| H2 | A panic in a root source, trace, size or weak source callback during `collect` resets the heap state, not a poisoned heap: `UnmarkOnUnwind` clears every mark bit (small pages and LOS) and the pending work lists are dropped. The lazy sweep holds a per-page guard and stores the VO word before it zaps | high | `heap.rs` (`UnmarkOnUnwind`: nothing is freed before marking ends, so clean marks are the whole state); tests `caught_{root_source,trace,weak_source}_panic_leaves_no_marks`, `caught_size_panic_in_lazy_sweep_leaves_no_marks` | A collect step frees memory before marking ends | The heap stays usable after a caught panic; the cost is on the panic path only |
| H3 | A new small page takes a cached (committed) free chunk first, else the lowest free chunk. At most 16 freed small pages stay cached (`MAX_CACHED_FREE_PAGES`). A large object evicts cached chunks in its run, and others when the limit needs it | high | `crates/ep9_heap/src/memory.rs`; tests `cached_page_is_reused_under_the_limit`, `cached_pages_never_cause_out_of_memory` | — | Before: a tight `max_heap_bytes` gave a false out-of-memory |
| Fr1 | Pre-scan of regex classes: without the v flag a class is one level; with v every unescaped `[` opens a class and every `]` closes one. The scan keeps both counts and picks one by the flags | high | `crates/ep9_frontend/src/prescan.rs`; unit test and regex gate forms at cap, cap+1 and 1M levels; e2e through `ep9` | — | Before: `/[[a]&&[[a]&&...]]/v` at 100k levels aborted with a stack overflow instead of a RangeError |
| Fr2 | Names bound by a catch pattern are TDZ bindings, initialized at the end of the pattern (as non-simple parameters); BytecodeGen stores the hole first. A plain `catch (e)` has no TDZ | high | test `scope::tests::catch_patterns_have_a_tdz`; e2e `exceptions/catch_pattern_tdz.js` (output from node) | — | ECMA-262 CatchClauseEvaluation |
| Fr3 | IteratorClose on a throw completion: the `return` lookup and the call run under the handler that ignores errors, so the original exception stays (ECMA-262 7.4.11 step 4). The normal path keeps both errors | high | test `bytecodegen::tests::iterator_close_on_a_throw_ignores_errors_of_the_return_lookup`; e2e `iter/iterator_close_return.js`; 9 goldens, 3 more instructions per IteratorClose | — | The v0 VM has no accessors, so the getter case cannot run yet |
| Fr4 | `super[k]` keeps the key value; GetValue and PutValue convert it (`LoadKeyedFromSuper`, `StoreToSuper`). So `super[k] = v` converts after `v` runs. Compound, logical assignment and update convert once, before the read | high | test `super_keys_are_converted_by_get_value_and_put_value`; e2e `classes/super_key_order.js`; test262 `target-super-computed-reference.js` passes | — | Spec and JSC (bun 1.4.3); node 24 converts twice, ep9 does not follow node here |
| Fr5 | `(o?.m)()` and `` (o?.m)`x` `` keep `o` as the receiver: a parenthesized chain that ends in a property access is still a reference. A nullish base gives an undefined callee, and the call throws a TypeError after the arguments run | high | golden `call-optional-paren`; e2e `objects/optional_call_receiver.js`; test262 `optional-call-preserves-this.js` passes | — | |
| Fr6 | Corpus checks assert their claims and fail when a third_party checkout is missing or empty (the message names the sync script): scanner, scope pass (mismatches exactly SplitBody 54, AnnexB 488, CatchVar 4), P4, `measure_forms`, bytecodegen corpus | high | `crates/ep9_frontend/src/probe.rs` (`assert_checkout`); commit `2111b19` | A pin bump changes a count: the PR that bumps updates the numbers | Before: they printed problems and passed with 0 files |
| P1 | `EhFrameRegistration::register` Safety: drop a registration only when no thread unwinds or walks through that code, and the spans (lowest `pc_begin` to highest FDE end) of two live registrations must not overlap. Doc-only | high | `crates/ep9_platform/src/unwind/unix.rs`; probes: a drop during a walk gives SIGSEGV in libunwind (macOS arm64), abort or SIGSEGV with libgcc 12 (Linux arm64, x64); interleaved spans lose a frame with libgcc 12 and 14 | — | **Open, unverified:** the Windows `FunctionTableRegistration` contract has no drop-during-walk rule and likely has the same race |
| P2 | `native_return_addresses(0)` returns no frame (the doc says "at most `max`") | high | test `unwind::tests::native_return_addresses_honors_max` (commit `9d2a0fb`) | — | Bug fix, listed for completeness |

### 10.3 H15 on T0 (x86-64 release dispatch)

Data: [measurements/2026-10-21-t0-dispatch-x64.md](measurements/2026-10-21-t0-dispatch-x64.md). Decision record: [adr/H15-interpreter-dispatch.md](adr/H15-interpreter-dispatch.md). Trigger: the §7 H15-X64 reopen rule "the x64 loop check fails on T0" fired on `ep9_vm` (157 opcodes, 282 handlers).

| ID | Decision | Conf. | Evidence | Reopen rule | Note |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| H15-X64-T0 | Release expansion: sibling calls on every target (`Dispatch::RELEASE = Sibling`). Debug: match everywhere | high | T0 match loop on x86-64: 2 indirect jumps (need ≥ 79), 1 of them the dispatch; 92-134 stack accesses away from calls. Match / sibling time, geomean of 4 T0 kernels: 1.21-1.47 on 22 hosted x86-64 jobs (EPYC 7763, 9V74, 9V45; Xeon 8370C, 8573C); all 110 kernel results have the interval above 1 (lowest bound 1.037). aarch64: 1.26-1.53. cachegrind: match runs 1.09-1.63x the instructions | On a named x64 model T0 match beats T0 sibling (PERF-NOISE fail, geomean of 4 T0 kernels, ≥ 31 rounds); a T0 sibling handler loses its tail jump on a tier-1 target and no DSL fix restores it; the release expansion fails against cgoto.c on a named x64 model | **Supersedes §7 H15-X64.** The spike's x86-64 result (match faster on Zen 3 and Zen 4) came from its threaded 20-opcode loop and does not hold for T0 |
| H15-HANDLER-ABI | On x86-64 the handlers are `extern "sysv64-unwind"` on every OS (`dsl::handler!`). Other targets keep the Rust ABI. The ≤ 4 argument rule stays | high | Win64 fast handlers with a frame: 34 → 12 (Linux: 11). Windows sibling time old / new: 1.13 (EPYC 7763), 1.04 (Xeon 8573C), 1.02 (EPYC 9V45); Linux control 0.996, 1.003; `ep9_vm` and `ep9_cli` tests pass on windows-2025 (run 39503403036) | A Windows tool cannot walk or unwind these frames; the gain is gone on a named model (old / new ≤ 1) | `-unwind` keeps a panic (a VM bug) unwinding. Nightly `become` builds with it |
| H15-NO-THREADED-T0-MATCH | No change that makes the T0 match loop threaded: no `unreachable_unchecked()` for a bad opcode and no global `-tail-dup-*` LLVM flags. For T0, `--match-loop` is a report | high | Probes (measurements §3): each change alone keeps 2 dispatch jumps; both together give 251 jumps but 320 stack accesses away from calls. The first is UB on a VM bug; the flags apply to the whole binary | A release expansion with a match loop is proposed again (then H15-Q2 first) | The spike's threaded-loop gate stays (SP-Dispatch only, DISPATCH-GATE (T0)) |
| XTASK-MACHO-DICE | `cargo xtask disasm-check` drops the "instructions" that `llvm-objdump -d` decodes from Mach-O data-in-code ranges (LC_DATA_IN_CODE): x86-64 jump tables in `__text` are data. In a linked image the base is the vmaddr of the segment at file offset 0 | high | x86_64-apple-darwin: `ep9loop__match__run` 8 → 2 jumps (as Linux, Windows); the SP-Dispatch loop 17 → 16; tests `macho_jump_table_is_data`, `macho_data_in_code_base` | The `llvm-objdump --data-in-code` output changes | Tool fix; listed because check results depend on it |
| H15-Q1 | Answered early: the x64 release expansion with the T0 opcode set is sibling calls, on 5 named x64 models | medium | H15 ADR, Open questions | — | **Still open:** one Intel client model and a gate-host run, by M2 exit (T0 parity) |
| DISPATCH-GATE (T0) | The §7 gate per target, with "release expansion" = sibling calls on every target. x86-64 speed gates: `sibling-vs-cgoto-x64-sysv`, `-x64-win64` (≤ 1.25x cgoto.c geomean, ≤ 1.5x per kernel). Reports, never gates: `match-vs-cgoto` on every target, `sibling-vs-match` on x86-64. The spike's x86-64 `--match-loop` shape stays a blocking structural gate (H15 Rules: the threaded-loop rule applies to SP-Dispatch, not to T0) | medium | `spikes/sp-dispatch` (`gate.sh`, `src/main.rs`, `src/vm.rs`); local gate on Apple M5 Max (load 9-13, provisional): exit 0, every structural gate passes (aarch64; static x64 SysV and Win64, negatives included), `sibling-vs-cgoto-a64` 0.985, `sibling-vs-match-a64` 1.550 | As §7 DISPATCH-GATE (amended) | **Supersedes §7 DISPATCH-GATE (amended)** (note). Speed gates still never block on hosted CI. H15-Q3 (cgoto.c compiler pin) stays open |

## 11. 2026-10-21: M0 week 4

**Source.** The M0 week-4 build, run by the orchestrator under the user's delegation of 2026-10-20 (§5). A design workflow (3 designers, a planner and a critic) wrote the build plan; the critic's corrected plan is the one used. The plan runs in phases P0-P4 on branch `m0/wk4`; each phase merges its agent branches, passes CI, gets a Codex adversarial review and appends its rows here.

```text
week-4 plan (design + critic)        -> 11.1
P0 scaffold (VM split, crates, ADRs) -> 11.2
process (CI blocked, local CI)        -> 11.3
P1 (shapes, strings, ABI, math, unit) -> 11.4
P1 review (Codex) and its fixes       -> 11.5
phase numbers (test262, BENCH)        -> 11.6
x86-64 sandbox (real hardware CI)     -> 11.7
P2 (objects, symbols, tables, lint)   -> 11.8
```

### 11.1 Week-4 plan decisions

| ID | Decision | Reason | Evidence |
|---|---|---|---|
| W4-BASE | Base is m0/followups at 7eca206, which includes fx/exit70 (eb20c38, 8fdf4da). Every worktree links third_party/{test262,bench-v8v7,bench-octane,bench-jetstream,shells} from the main repo after a pin check | fx/exit70 fixes the random cachegrind exit 70; test262, the corpus digest and v8v7 need the checkouts; VM file:line refs are unchanged | git diff --stat 942d782 7eca206 touches only crates/ep9_platform and tools/bench; crates/ep9_platform/src/va.rs:43; main repo third_party/test262 at d53ebc8b, bench-v8v7 at c6453cd |
| W4-ABI-NONE | No bytecode ABI bump in week 4. FeedbackSlotKind keeps bytes 0-20 and its names as H16 v0; RuntimeId stays a placeholder | The fingerprint hashes the opcode text, the tiny unit and the runtime table, not doc text; no week-4 item needs a new opcode, kind or id | crates/ep9_bytecode/tests/abi.rs:16-24; abi-history.txt `2 6d92eb9a`; decisions-log BCG-CORPUS decb0871…8fce76 |
| W4-RUNTIMEID | RuntimeId has 19 ids (0-18). Accessors use the existing ids 12 and 16 | Corrects the stale brief (6 ids) | crates/ep9_bytecode/src/feedback.rs:129-182; crates/ep9_vm/src/interp/slow.rs:705 |
| W4-ABI-STEWARD | P2-FACTS owns crates/ep9_bytecode/src/feedback.rs and alone may bump the ABI, at most once, in P4 (default none) | One owner for one bump | task brief; H02 change-log rules |
| W4-PHASES | 5 phases with 3/7/6/6/3 agents and disjoint paths. The unit header carries the page size as its own field | I54 | crates/ep9_heap/src/heap.rs:434; crates/ep9_heap/src/trace.rs:362; crates/ep9_platform/src/jit/tests.rs |
| W4-H11-Q3 | H11-Q3 filled: the barrier version is one counter (BARRIER_VERSION = 2), bumped by review, carried as a u32 at unit header offset 100; a mismatch gives BYTECODE | I54 | crates/ep9_heap/src/barrier.rs:50; tests/check.rs every_header_field_mutation_gives_its_listed_outcome |
| W4-PERF-LIB | The PERF-NOISE statistics live in tools/bench/lib/stats.mjs and the host and load-guard code in lib/host.mjs. t0-dispatch.mjs and cross/run.mjs import them. The shared verdict(ratios, threshold) returns above / below / marginal / insufficient data, and t0-dispatch maps these to its old labels | one copy of the gate rules (PERF-3) | lib/stats.mjs; t0-dispatch.test.mjs (old tests unchanged, plus a test that the functions are the shared ones) |
| W4-PERF-RATIO | A cross ratio is t(engine)/t(ep9) per round, i.e. ep9's speed relative to that engine, the plan's '0.25x QuickJS' convention. The verdict uses net ratios (each time minus the same engine's empty-script time in the same round); gross ratios are shown too. The canary reading is richards and deltablue against 0.6x QuickJS | engine start-up differs: empty script 2.4 ms (QuickJS) vs 21.2 ms (node) | docs/measurements/2026-10-21-perf-v0.md, times table |
| W4-PERF-ORDER | Each round rotates the workload order. Per workload, the engine order is a Latin square (offset = round + workload index), so over E rounds each engine runs in each position once. An output-check pass runs every file on every engine before any timing | no engine always runs first or after a given workload | run.test.mjs rotation test; the CLI test compares the fake engine's call log with the plan |
| W4-PERF-REFUSE | A run with a nonzero exit or stdout different from the expected output stops the runner: REFUSED, exit 1, nothing written. Expected outputs are the T0 kernels' own `want` (a test keeps the table and the files in step) and '<name> <N>' per v8v7 benchmark | a wrong answer must never pass as a fast run | run.test.mjs refusal tests |
| W4-PERF-GUARD | Load guard: settle 60 s, then load <= idle + 0.5; poll up to 30 min; check again 120 s after the run. If the host file has no idle baseline, the guard cannot pass and the runner does not wait. Without --allow-busy the runner stops with 'host busy' (exit 3). A host file whose CPU or platform does not match the host is a usage error | 10-quality section 3 load guard | lib/host.mjs; run.test.mjs guard tests |
| W4-PERF-HOST | tools/bench/hosts/m5max-macos.toml lists the CPU, cores (6 Super + 12 Performance), memory, OS, power and gate_host = true, but has no idle baseline. It is to be measured with node tools/bench/cross/idle.mjs in a quiet window | the rule needs 10 idle minutes; only 3 quiet minutes were seen | host file partial_idle_samples (08:58-09:01 +08) |
| W4-PERF-STUB | v8v7.mjs reads each kernel from the pinned git blob (cat-file) and refuses a checkout at another HEAD or with a changed file. It copies base.js's deterministic Math.random only where Math exists. Each benchmark runs setup, then run() N times, then tear-down, and prints '<name> <N>'. No date is written, so the output is the same every time | determinism; the same text on every engine (print falls back to console.log) | two runs compare equal (cmp); runs on all 5 engines |
| W4-PERF-N | Default N per kernel: richards 200 (from the plan), deltablue 100, crypto 8, raytrace 24, earley-boyer 8, regexp 5, splay 200, navier-stokes 12. Each gives QuickJS about 0.35 s, like richards | comparable run lengths for run 2 and later | QuickJS time per run() = (t(N=21) - t(N=1))/20, best of 3, 2026-10-21 (comments in v8v7.mjs) |
| W4-PERF-RUN1 | Run 1 (provisional), ep9 vs QuickJS, net: richards 0.239x (0.228-0.245), t0-call 0.374x (0.367-0.390), t0-prop 0.449x (0.431-0.454), t0-branchy 1.458x, t0-loop 1.567x (void). Geomean of the 5 kernels: 0.620x QuickJS, 0.487x node --jitless. ep9 retires 45.37G instructions on richards vs 6.63G for QuickJS | first perf report | docs/measurements/2026-10-21-perf-v0.{md,json} |

### 11.5 P1 review: Codex findings and fixes

Codex (gpt-6.1-sol) reviewed the P1 diff (d1c3f4c..f650916) in slices under 1 MB: tools (357 KB), then the engine in two parts, heap + math (128 KB) and ep9_vm (283 KB). The first whole-engine run (421 KB) stopped early but gave one concrete claim (SoftFma). Each finding was checked by a separate agent that read the control flow, reproduced it and ran a negative control. Local CI on 2c7c566 also found a rustdoc failure that no P1 agent had run.

```text
finding                                   class  result
SoftFma loses a sticky bit                 b     fixed 7ca6ef8 (upstream libm bug too)
fp-check misses code after Mach-O tables   b     fixed ef14657
perf canary passes on invalid net time     b     fixed 76e66ab
unit keeps hints on a combined mismatch    b     fixed bb59feb
heap: false OOM across sweep queues        b     fixed f5f04e9 (+2 older LOS/medium cases, same arm)
string_view dangles after a shared GC      b     fixed 4bfa87f
to_object succeeds through the stub        b     fixed bf69589
non-extensible bypass on define paths      c     same code at d1c3f4c; split to P2-OBJ, P2-BTAB, P3-ACC, P3-IC
rustdoc: [[...]] read as links             b     fixed f227ec1
```

| ID | Decision | Reason | Evidence |
|---|---|---|---|
| P1FIX-FMA-STICKY | Fix the SoftFma sticky bit in ep9 (musl's one expression: the sticky bit comes from the old `rlo`), not wait for upstream | H36 allows a hardware/software dispatch only between exact implementations. libm 0.2.16 has the same bug (rust-lang/compiler-builtins#1353, open, no fix) | 4 concrete inputs, exact rational check; soft_vs_hw 10M: 19,651 mismatches before, 0 after |
| P1FIX-FMA-CORPUS | soft_vs_hw gets near-tie inputs plus a fixed near-tie grid test | Random inputs never hit "tie plus a far tail": the old corpus reached the branch 2,169,912 times with 82,435 wrong sticky bits but 0 changed results | soft_vs_hw_near_ties: 1,852 of 168,960 differ before, 0 after |
| FPC-1 | fp-check reuses disasm.rs `redecode_after_data`, `drop_data` and `parse_data_in_code` (now pub(crate)) | One boundary-recovery path for both checks | disasm-check stdout identical before and after; fixture jump-table-fma fails before, passes after |
| FPC-2 | Re-decode by address (start = table end, stop = next table or next symbol), not by symbol name | Demangled names contain commas and --disassemble-symbols takes a comma list | ep9 x86_64-apple-darwin object: 406,332 instructions equal to `--macho -d`, 0 differences |
| FPC-3 | Re-decode after every table | Simpler; +8.7 s on x86_64-apple-darwin only | 11.2 s -> 20.0 s |
| W4-PERF-NETINVALID | A row with a net time <= 0 in any round is "net invalid" and provisional; its canary reading is "no gate verdict"; gross values stay shown, marked "(gross)" | Gates read net ratios only (W4-PERF-RATIO) | run.test.mjs regression test fails before, passes after; run-1 report unchanged (smallest net time 103.4 ms) |
| W4-AOTU-HINTS | `Verdict::Version` carries `drop_hints`, set from the fact schema on its own; the verdict still names the first difference in header order | Moving the fact-schema check first would change the pinned layout text and need a container bump | a_fact_schema_mismatch_drops_the_hints_whatever_else_differs fails before, passes after |
| W4-HEAP-OOM-SWEEP | If an allocation still fails after its GC, `Inner::sweep_before_out_of_memory` (cold) sweeps both lazy queues and the allocation retries once without a second GC; same arm in `alloc_slow` and `alloc_shape` | Both spaces share max_heap_bytes; a page that died in this GC waited in the other space's queue, giving OutOfMemory with 0 marked cells. verify_after_gc hid it | 5 tests fail before, pass after (debug, release, Miri); all 7 functions that inline Heap::alloc are unchanged; cachegrind ci-v0 +3,116 Ir (+0.004%) |
| W4-HEAP-OOM-SWEEP-SCOPE | The same arm also fixes the older LOS and medium-cell cases | One cold arm, no hot-path change; dropping them would need extra code to exclude them | Both tests fail on d1c3f4c |
| W4-STR-VIEW-EXCL | `Isolate::string_view` takes `&mut self` | The exclusive borrow blocks every isolate call (global, heap().collect/alloc, run_script). A rooting guard would need Drop and would not survive a moving GC. The API has no caller yet | Probe: 16 of 16 views read freed text after heap().collect(); 2 new compile_fail doctests (E0502), the collect_garbage one is now E0499 |
| W4-P1FIX-WRAPPER-NI | Until P2-SYM merges, `Cx::to_object` and `Cx::new_wrapper` throw not_implemented("wrapper objects for primitives") for primitives | A success that drops the primitive gives wrong answers silently (length undefined); not_implemented is the v0 rule | tests/abi.rs object_ops_and_conversions |
| W4-P1-EXT-SPLIT | The non-extensible and descriptor bypasses are class (c) and go to their plan owners: P2-OBJ (define paths, prevent_extensions on shapes), P2-BTAB (GlobalDeclarationInstantiation), P3-ACC (CreateGlobalFunctionBinding, DefineMethodProperty, SetPrototypeInLiteral), P3-IC (add-property handlers miss on non-extensible receivers) | The unchecked paths are the same at d1c3f4c. Nothing in JS can make an object non-extensible in week 4, but `function NaN(){}` and `static ['prototype'](){}` reach the descriptor half today | scratchpad week4/p1-ext-handoff.md; acceptance tests in probes-ext/acceptance |
| W4-P1-RUSTDOC | Spec notation in doc comments goes in backticks (`` `[[GetOwnProperty]]` ``); every agent prompt from P2 on lists `RUSTDOCFLAGS="-D warnings" cargo doc --workspace --no-deps --locked` | Local CI failed on 11 doc lines that no P1 agent had checked | Local CI rustdoc step, f227ec1 |

### 11.7 x86-64 Linux on a Cloudflare sandbox

**Source.** The user, 2026-10-21: "If you want to debug & test x86 code path, you can use Cloudflare sandbox", then "cross compile locally via cargo-zigbuild and test it on Cloudflare sandbox, use a large instance".

```text
Mac (arm64)                                     Cloudflare sandbox ep9-x86-sandbox (standard-4)
cargo zigbuild --target                          AMD EPYC Zen 4, 4 vCPU, 12 GiB, 20 GB, glibc 2.41
  x86_64-unknown-linux-gnu.2.39   --- sbx --->   same absolute paths as the Mac
  + real libgcc_s.so.1                           tests, test262, math, spikes, perf CI v0
```

| ID | Decision | Reason | Evidence |
|---|---|---|---|
| W4-X86-SANDBOX | A dedicated harness `infra/x86-sandbox` (Worker ep9-x86-sandbox, account 6db7cbc0..., scheduling policy durable_object, Registry cap 2 running ids, Bearer token over HTTPS only), copied from the proven webcodecs harness. Size standard-4 | The account limit is 4 vCPU / 12,288 MiB / 20,000 MB per deployment (GET containers/me, 2026-10-21), so standard-4 is the largest allowed. A separate Worker keeps ep9 out of the webcodecs slots | Smoke test: auth 401/403 checks, 20 MB put/get sha256 match, 200 s bg job, cap 429 on a 3rd id; all ids destroyed |
| W4-X86-LIBGCC | Every x86 binary links the real Debian libgcc_s.so.1 (pinned sha256) with target x86_64-unknown-linux-gnu.2.39 | zig links LLVM libunwind by default; ep9's linux-gnu JIT unwind registers the whole .eh_frame the libgcc way, so jit::tests::unwind_through_generated_frame and sp-jit-mem failed under it. glibc 2.39 is what that libgcc_s needs and equals GitHub ubuntu-24.04 | zig-linked build: 2 unwind failures; libgcc-linked: 19/19 pass on the box; a build step checks all 57 ELFs |
| W4-X86-LOCALCI | local-ci.sh drops the Rosetta docker-amd64 step and runs scratchpad ci/x86-sandbox.sh in the background from the start (about 13 min) | Real hardware runs the 2 tests Rosetta had to skip (W4-ROSETTA-SKIPS is no longer needed), uses real vfmadd, and adds test262 parse, GC-stress exec, the spike binaries and perf CI v0 on Linux x86-64 | First full run on c72d6bc: 604 passed / 0 failed / 17 ignored (35 binaries), test262 exec failures 43,839 new 0, GC-stress exec 0 infra errors, soft_vs_hw 10M 0 mismatches vs vfmadd, cachegrind ci-v0 82,970,298 Ir |
| W4-COMMIT-PATHS | In the shared main checkout, every commit names its paths (`git commit -- <paths>`) | c72d6bc ("docs: decisions-log 11.5 ...") was made with a plain `git commit` while the harness agent had 11 infra/x86-sandbox files staged, so it holds them too. It is not rewritten: the six P2 branches start from it. No secret was committed (.token and .url are git-ignored; checked with git ls-files) | `git show --stat c72d6bc` |

Open (product call, not week 4): `crates/ep9_platform/src/unwind/unix.rs` sets WHOLE_SECTION from `target_env = "gnu"`, which assumes libgcc. A linux-gnu build linked to LLVM libunwind loses JIT frames and aborts a panic through JIT code with no error message. Guard or document before Spike B (H23).

| W4-CI-PERF-RUNS | 3 runs, 0 warm-up per workload, workloads counted in parallel (--jobs = CPU count) | with the seed fixed, runs agree within about 1e-7, so more runs only add time. | W4-CI-PERF-CHECK | each perf workload first gets one plain ep9 run, which must exit 0 and print the expected output (the cross runner's refusal rule), else it gets no count and the step exits 1. | W4-CI-T262-TIMEOUT | the test262 linux-x64 job gets a 120 min timeout, the other runners stay at 60 | GC-stress + eager costs about 18 CPU-minutes on the M5 Max. No test is near the 60 s soft timeout | Docker Linux arm64: wall 71 s, user 1067 s at 18 workers; --timeout 15 gives 0 timeouts |

P3-ACC rows:

| ID | Decision | Reason | Evidence |
|---|---|---|---|
| W4-ACC-PAIR | ACCESSOR_PAIR (kind 67, 24 B) never changes. Each define builds a new pair from the old halves plus the descriptor (JSC style). A pair someone has read stays a true snapshot. | A getter that redefines its own property cannot change a pair that a caller already read; no pair is shared mutable state | tests/accessors.rs validate_and_apply_accessor_descriptors |
| W4-ACC-EPOCH | replacing a pair on a prototype calls shape::note_change, which bumps proto_epoch. An IC that caches a getter or setter found on a prototype stays valid while the epoch holds. | P3-IC caches prototype holders by epoch; a pair swap is a structural change | replacing_an_accessor_of_a_prototype_bumps_the_epoch |
| W4-ACC-ENGINE-TEXT | engine-made text (error_header for .stack, constructor_name) reads data properties only and never calls a getter. V8 also does not run JS while it builds a stack trace header in this path. | A user getter ran inside throw_error (also on stack overflow) and the debug CLI panicked with "host function returned Ok with a pending exception" | engine_error_text_runs_no_getters |
| W4-ACC-PRIM-SET | [[Set]] on a primitive walks the prototype of its wrapper with the primitive as the receiver (PutValue 6.a). A setter receives the primitive as this; checked against node. | §13.15.2 PutValue 6.a and §10.1.9.2 pass the primitive as the receiver | getters_and_setters_receive_the_receiver_also_a_primitive (node 24 output) |
| W4-ACC-ABI-TEST | the tests/abi.rs not-implemented assertion is replaced by a positive assertion (same kind as W4-P2-EXC-TEXT). | The old assertion tested that accessors are missing | crates/ep9_vm/src/tests/abi.rs |
| W4-ACC-INLINE | hot property helpers are #[inline(always)]; getter and setter calls, set_primitive and fail are #[cold] #[inline(never)]; get_primitive is #[inline(never)]. This keeps the fast path the same size under fat LTO with codegen-units=1. | The new call sites moved these helpers out of line under fat LTO: t0-prop +10.6% | BENCH t0-prop 0.9701x of base with EP9_HASH_SEED=1 |
| W4-ACC-ELEM | an accessor on an array index of an Array stays not_implemented('accessor array elements') until H18 element storage. Array length with an accessor descriptor returns false, as the spec says. | §10.4.2.1 elements with non-default attributes need H18 element storage (not week 4) | validate_and_apply_accessor_descriptors; 15.2.3.6-4-196/197 listed (W4-P3-FALSE-PASS) |

P3-BLTA rows:

| ID | Decision | Reason | Evidence |
|---|---|---|---|
| W4-BLTA-BOUND | A bound call walks the chain with loops (unwrap + chain_arg), never Rust recursion. this = innermost [[BoundThis]]. The args are innermost bound args, then each outer level's, then the call's own. new.target is replaced at each level where it equals that bound function. | §10.4.1.1/10.4.1.2 applied level by level gives exactly this. A 100000-deep chain must not overflow the native stack. | tests/function.rs bound chain test (100000 levels on an 8 MiB thread); subclass-of-bound and new.target tests match node |
| W4-BLTA-BOUNDARGS | [[BoundArguments]] lives in fixed field 2: UNDEFINED, or an internal dense ARRAY made with object::new_array plus array_set (RangeError over arr::MAX_LENGTH). | No new kind, the GC already traces arrays, and bind() with no args allocates nothing extra. | bound.rs; GC-stress tests in tests/function.rs |
| W4-BLTA-CALLSPLIT | call_with/construct_with dispatch FUNCTION vs BOUND_FUNCTION. The FUNCTION bodies moved into call_function_object/construct_function_object. slow::do_call splices the bound args into its arg Vec. | One FUNCTION body for all paths. The bytecode path already owns a Vec, so the splice is O(n) once. | call.rs, interp/slow/call.rs; UNIT(ep9_vm) 179 pass |
| W4-BLTA-FPTOSTRING | Function.prototype.toString returns the v0 NativeFunction form 'function NAME() { [native code] }' for every callable. NAME is the own data property `name` if it is an ASCII IdentifierName (an optional get/set prefix is allowed), else empty. A non-callable throws TypeError. | It removes 3 NEW FAILUREs. Source text is not kept in v0. The form matches the NativeFunction grammar (§20.2.3.5). | function.rs:200 `// v0:` marker; tests/function.rs toString test |
| W4-BLTA-INDEXOF-SPARSE | indexOf over more than 1024 indices visits only present index keys (own keys of O and its proto chain), and falls back to the plain loop if any of them is an accessor. | Each index key is an atom allocation, so a long sparse array cost one GC per hole under stress (T262-GC exit 2). No JS can run during the scan, so it is unobservable. | 15.4.4.14-10-1.js passes under gc-stress; tests/array.rs index_of_over_a_long_sparse_range |
| W4-BLTA-IDS | Append-only ids: object 1.0-1.5 (hasOwnProperty, defineProperty, getOwnPropertyDescriptor, getOwnPropertyNames, propertyIsEnumerable, keys); function 2.0-2.3 (call, apply, bind, toString); array 3.0-3.2 (isArray, pop, indexOf). | Template order first, then companions. | tests/golden/ids-{object,function,array}.txt |

## 12. M0 month 2

**Source.** The M0 month-2 build (weeks 5-8) and a month-3 outline to the M0 exit, run by the orchestrator under the user's delegation of 2026-10-20 (§5). A planning workflow wrote the build plan: 4 research agents, 3 designers ("arch", "t262", "gates"), a critic (30 issues, I1-I30) and a planner (73 decisions, 9 open items). A second workflow (6 checkers) checked the plan's claims against the tree at 55c15d0, and the orchestrator applied the result as plan v2. The plan runs in phases M2-P0 to M2-P5 on branch `m0/m2`; each phase merges its agent branches, passes local CI and the real CI, gets a Codex adversarial review and appends its rows here.

```text
month-2 plan (6 phases)               -> 12.1
planning decisions (73 rows)          -> 12.2
plan verification and its decisions   -> 12.3
repository and CI (public repo)       -> 12.4
open items at month start             -> 12.5
P0 scaffold                           -> 12.6
```

### 12.1 Plan

The month-2 build plan (kept outside the repo, like the week-4 plan) starts from the "gates" design and adds parts of the "arch" and "t262" designs (M2-BASE). The base is `m0/wk4` at 55c15d0. The integration branch is `m0/m2`, agent branches are `m0m2/<phase>-<role>`, and the orchestrator tags each phase merge `m2-p0` … `m2-p5`. Month 2 has 6 phases. P0 is a scaffold with no test262 change: ABI 3, FRAME 2, OBJECT_LAYOUT 2, the routing stubs and the Spike B protocol. P1-P3 build the Spike B prerequisites, Spike B and its bench, generators, classes, `with` and eval. Gate window 1 after P3 gives the D21 verdict, and SP-Unit starts in P4 only if the verdict is not "fail". P4 adds SP-Unit, Promise and the Array, Object and String builtins. P5 closes the month. A rolling Accept campaign (batches B1-B5b) runs through all phases. Month 3 (M3-P0..M3-P5 plus a W12 buffer) goes to the M0 exit run. Estimated test262 targets on macOS arm64: language/ ≥ 15,302 at P5 (base 10,884 / 23,726), and all dirs 25,170-26,190 (base 14,687 / 53,616).

```text
phase      main work                                                                 feature agents
P0         ABI 3, FRAME 2 + OBJECT_LAYOUT 2, routing stubs, Spike B protocol, ADRs   6
P1         CODE-RANGE, UNWIND-GUARD, NATIVE-ENTRY, ABORT-PRE, GEN-CG, GEN-VM,         7
           ABI-INDEX
P2         SPIKE-B, INFRA2 (panic = abort), CLS-CG, CLS-VM, LOOKUP, EVAL-FE,          7
           ACCEPT-1
P3         SPIKEB-BENCH, EVAL-VM, FNCTOR, ABI-SLOTS, ISOLATE-API, ADR-JIT, ACCEPT-2   7
             -> gate window 1 (D21 verdict)
P4         SP-UNIT (if the verdict is not "fail"), PROMISE, B-ARRAY, B-OBJECT,        7
           B-STRING, WIN-STATIC-1, ACCEPT-3
             -> gate window 2
P5 close   B-REFLECT-GLOBALS, VM-SMALL, DOCS-SYNC, WIN-STATIC-2, ACCEPT-4, GATES     6

each phase also has the integration fixer and one Claude reviewer (9 agents or fewer)
```

Short paths are at 55c15d0: opcodes.rs, feedback.rs, unit.rs, serialize.rs and operand.rs are in crates/ep9_bytecode/src; call.rs, object.rs, kinds.rs, frame.rs, runtime.rs and natives.rs are in crates/ep9_vm/src.

### 12.3 Plan verification

Six checker agents (abi, vm, infra, p1, t262, process) checked 161 plan claims against the tree at 55c15d0. 63 claims are not true: 20 false, 42 partly true, 1 unverifiable. The orchestrator handled all 63 in plan v2: 59 applied, 3 merged with another finding, and 1 (infra#6, billing) replaced by the public-repo change (§12.4). An id `checker#n` counts only the not-true findings of that checker, in checker order.

| Checker | Claims | Not true | Blocker | High | Medium | Low |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| abi | 24 | 10 | 1 | 2 | 3 | 4 |
| vm | 30 | 9 | 0 | 1 | 2 | 6 |
| infra | 24 | 8 | 0 | 1 | 4 | 3 |
| p1 | 27 | 9 | 1 | 1 | 4 | 3 |
| t262 | 26 | 11 | 1 | 0 | 4 | 6 |
| process | 30 | 16 | 1 | 1 | 10 | 4 |
| total | 161 | 63 | 4 | 6 | 27 | 26 |

Blocker and high findings:

| Id | Sev. | Plan v1 said | What is true | Fix in plan v2 |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| abi#0 | blocker | P0-LANG can add CallRuntimeForPair 0x9d and pass BUILD alone | ep9_vm makes one handler per opcode of the opcode list, so ep9_vm does not build without a handler (crates/ep9_vm/src/interp/ops.rs:1; interp/dsl.rs:477-478) | M2-PAIR-GLUE, M2-P0VM-TWO-STEP |
| abi#1 | high | RegOutPair is an existing operand kind | Kinds are 0-19 only; there is RegPair (read) and RegOutTriple, no RegOutPair (operand.rs:69-113) | `OperandKind::RegOutPair = 20` and its arms (M2-ABI3) |
| abi#4 | high | The digest changes only in a codegen phase | The unit hash covers ABI_VERSION (serialize.rs:401, :365-367), so ABI 3 changes the digest | BCG-CORPUS-4 after the LANG merge (M2-DETERMINISM) |
| vm#0 | high | A pointer to the unit's `Scope` lets LOOKUP read slot names | ScopeSlot.name is a unit StringId (unit.rs:359-361); only the loaded unit maps it to a string (crates/ep9_vm/src/unit.rs:143-145) | `ContextScope { unit, scope }` record (M2-CTX-SCOPE-PTR) |

| M2-P1-NE-CALLANY | call_any(cx, fp, off, acc) decodes the call at off with no heap copy. A native callee gets a nested enter_native (JIT->JIT works without touching call.rs, which is GEN-VM's). Construct, bound, host and T0 callees go through call::call_with / construct_with. Spread is not_implemented. | One emit pattern serves every Call* opcode, matching T0's slow::call(cx, fp, pc, acc). There is no allocation on JIT->T0 calls (call::decode_call allocates a Vec). | t0_calls_native_code_and_native_code_calls_back rows g(inc1), g(print), g(bump), keep(g, bump) |
| M2-P1-NE-NOFEEDBACK | A T0->native entry neither counts the call nor makes a feedback vector. It still decrements cx.budget, as T0 entries do. | The spike JIT records no feedback (plan-v3 §5 P2-SPIKE-B), and skipping this means one less GC point. | slow/call.rs call_native |
| M2-P1-NE-STACKCHECK | The do_call native branch has no native stack check. call_any checks native_stack_ok before a nested native entry; nested T0 entries are checked in call_function_object; the JIT prologue compares Cx::native_limit. | One compare in the prologue (H23-Q5), and every T0<->native cycle goes through a check. | deep_t0_native_recursion_is_a_catchable_range_error (each dispatch) |
| M2-P1-NE-PANIC | No catch_unwind in table functions. A panic stops at the extern "C"/"sysv64" boundary and aborts. | SPIKE-B check (h) needs an abort even in an unwind build. Catching would turn a VM bug into a JS exception in an unknown state. | a_panic_under_native_code_aborts: the child exits with SIGABRT and its stderr contains 'panic in a function that cannot unwind' |
| M2-P1-NE-API | install_native refuses: class constructors, non-bytecode values, kinds other than Baseline/AotBaseline, an already-native function, and registry overlap. It writes the shared FunctionInfo slot and registers a CodeEntry. uninstall_native restores Interp and unregisters. | H14: one slot write, plus a registry entry for unwinding and stack traces. | native_slots_and_the_code_registry |
| M2-P1-NE-OUTFP | Set cx.out_fp / out_pc after the native call returns | A nested dispatch inside call_any overwrites them. Found when the first test run returned early ('!a' instead of 'done'). | slow/call.rs call_native |
| M2-P1-ABORT-GROUPS | Parse workers reply in groups of up to 8, or sooner when no request waits. The parent keeps up to 2 groups in flight. When a worker ends with more than 1 file unanswered, each of those files runs again alone; the file that ends a worker again gets the error. If none does, the first file of the group gets it, with the note 'none stopped it again'. | One reply per file cost +15% CPU and +7-13% wall on the full parse. Groups bring it to +1-5% wall. Running the files again alone keeps the crash on the right file, and a crash is never lost. | scratchpad/abortpre/logs/bench-parse.txt; tests a_crash_that_does_not_repeat_alone_is_still_an_infra_error and parse_worker_crash_is_an_infra_error_and_the_run_goes_on |
| M2-P1-ABORT-HARD | Parse hard timeout: 120 s per reply. On timeout the worker is killed and the file is an infrastructure error ('timeout: hard timeout...'). | A stuck parse worker would otherwise hang the CI job. A file parses in milliseconds. | parse.rs HARD_TIMEOUT |
| M2-P1-ABORT-NOUNWIND | Two test hooks, nounwind-panic and parse-nounwind-panic, panic inside an extern "C" frame. The hook runs, then the process aborts, the same way a panic = "abort" build ends. | This lets P1 tests prove now that the panic text reaches the infra line through stderr, before P2-INFRA2 turns on abort. | claims.log claim 2; tests a_panic_that_aborts_the_worker_reaches_the_report (parse, exec) |
| M2-P1-ABORT-CLI-HOOK | The ep9 panic hook exits 70 from the panicking thread, the front-end worker included. It prints the plan's line, then 'ep9: panicked at <loc> on thread <name>'. It prints a backtrace only when RUST_BACKTRACE asks for one. | Any panic is an ep9 bug. Exiting inside the hook keeps the status at 70 under panic = "abort" (abort gives 134). No ep9 code catches a panic on purpose (grep of catch_unwind: only worker.rs, spill.rs, unwind/unix.rs, each of which resumes it). | claims.log extra check (hook exit=70 vs 134); cli.rs a_panic_is_an_internal_error_with_exit_70 |
| M2-P1-ABORT-H23-KIND | I logged the H23 change as 'clarify (as built)': new Facts rows and ref moves; no Decision or Rules row changes. | README 3.3 kind rules; plan C15 'as built' label. The reviewer may reclassify it as fill, because the rows record the W4-PANIC-ABORT pre-switch work. | docs/adr/H23-limits-and-failure-policy.md change log |
| M2-P1-ABORT-H22-STABLE | No exec.rs line above :566 was added or removed. | H22:45 and :46 point to exec.rs:559-565 and :50-53, and H22 belongs to another agent. | ADRLINE 0 FIX |
| M2-P1-ABI-INDEX-NOATOM | The index ops find an element, a String wrapper index or a missing index without a key. A fast shape holds no array-index key (OM-10). Stored integer keys of an object with <=64 own props are scanned (array_index_of, or the digits for >= 2^32-1). A missing array index stops at the receiver while !shape::proto_index_keys. Else number_key (atom) and the ordinary path | An atom per index made indexOf on a long sparse range cost one GC per hole under stress (W4-BLTA-INDEXOF-SPARSE). There is no lookup-without-insert outside cfg(test) (atoms::lookup, atoms.rs not owned) | tests::abi_index (atom count unchanged over 10^4 calls; agreement with the key path on 7 kinds x 14 indices; 3 mutation runs fail the tests); tests/array.rs 6.0 s -> 1.4 s |
| M2-P1-INDEXOF-SCAN-REMOVED (amends W4-BLTA-INDEXOF-SPARSE and W4-P3FIX-INDEXOF-ALLKEYS) | indexOf runs the plain spec loop over has_index/get_index. present_indices, SPARSE_SCAN_MIN and canonical_index are removed | A hole needs no atom now, so the scan is not needed. 15.4.4.14-10-1 passes under GC stress | T262-GC 0 new failure; tests/array.rs index_of_over_a_long_sparse_range and index_of_finds_integer_keys_past_the_array_index_range pass unchanged |
| M2-P1-ABI-INDEX-U64 | The index is a u64. The key is ! ToString(𝔽(index)); above 2^53-1 it goes through f64 (key path) | LengthOfArrayLike gives u64 up to 2^53-1 | test index_ops_agree_with_the_key_path covers 2^53-1, 2^53, 2^53+1, u64::MAX |
| M2-P1-ABI-DELETE-THROW | delete_index(o, k, throw) and set_index(o, k, v, throw) take a throw flag, like Cx::set | One method per op; DeletePropertyOrThrow = throw true | pop uses delete_index(o, newLen, true) |
| M2-P1-ABI-ITER-SYNC | get_iterator is sync only; the async form will be a separate addition (get_async_iterator) | A kind parameter whose async case throws would freeze an unbuilt shape into H20 | H20 follow-ups row |
| M2-P1-ABI-ITER-FROMMETHOD | get_iterator_from_method is public | Array.from / TypedArray.from call GetIteratorFromMethod after GetMethod | TEMPLATES 7, tests::abi_index get_iterator_errors |

While the isolate terminates it skips return | §7.4.11 step 5 | test iterator_close_with_a_throw_completion_keeps_the_exception (ok/bad/throw/non-callable return) |

| GV-2 | Saved values live in one ELEMENTS cell: [sent value (REGS_INPUT=0), params P, registers R], allocated once by CreateGeneratorObject | plan rule 8 (no Rust Vec of values), one precise GC-traced cell, no per-suspend allocation | object.rs new_generator/GenRegs; test a_suspended_generator_is_the_only_holder_of_an_object |

ResumeGenerator copies them back, then sets the saved slots and the input slot to undefined | a suspended generator must not keep dead values alive. ### 12.8 P2 plan (plan v4) and the P1 -> P2 hand-off

Plan v4 re-checks P2 against the P1 head 010a2e1: 4 checkers found 55 claims that were false or partly true, all applied; the editor took D1-D18 and the orchestrator D19-D22. The ownership check gives 0 overlaps between the 7 P2 roles. Merge order: ACCEPT-1 -> CLS-VM -> CLS-CG -> EVAL-FE -> LOOKUP -> SPIKE-B -> INFRA2. P2 base: m0/m2 after the Windows fix (44c5dcb) and this section.

| ID | Decision | Reason | Evidence |
|---|---|---|---|
| M2-H08-Q2 | No in M0. The 11 Spike B opcodes have no property load, and the spike JIT records no feedback. The RW-cell shape guard is tested in M1 (Spike C) | P2 orchestrator step 1 | plan v4 §5 |
| M2-P2-D1 | classes 2 asked for a new `Job` variant in bytecodegen/mod.rs (EVAL-FE's file). Instead CLS-CG pushes the two initializer functions as `Job::DefaultConstructor { class, id, name: None }` and `gen_default_constructor` (func.rs:1123, CLS-CG's grant) dispatches on `self.info.kind`. mod.rs stays EVAL-FE's alone; the variant doc (mod.rs:230) goes to the fixer | The variant already carries (class, id, name) (mod.rs:231-235); its arm calls `function_meta(cx, info, None, name)` (mod.rs:406-412), and `function_meta` already maps both initializer kinds to `K::ClassFieldsInitializer` (mod.rs:452-453). EVAL-FE needs mod.rs for the code kind (:331-333, :378-380), so a split would give the file 2 owners (§1 rule 1) | plan v4 (kept outside the repo) |
| M2-P2-D2 | classes 3 asked for ClassInfo fields and new BindingKind/ImplicitRef variants in scope/mod.rs (EVAL-FE's). Instead CLS-CG declares the brand and `.class-field-N` bindings in `visit_class` as `BindingKind::PrivateName` under names no source can spell (e.g. `#.brand`), found by name in `ScopeInfo::bindings`. If that fails, CLS-CG stops and reports | resolve.rs:443-446 puts every PrivateName binding in the class context with no reference needed; `ScopeInfo.bindings` (scope/mod.rs:320) lists the class scope's bindings with names; `ClassInfo` already holds both initializer ids (:298-301). EVAL-FE needs scope/mod.rs for `ScopeKind::Eval` (:248-268) and the analysis entry. lookup-eval 5 proposed the same rule ("or uses an existing kind") | plan v4 (kept outside the repo) |

With CLS-CG first, no merged tree has a failing test; only EVAL-FE's own branch fails that one test before its rebase. ### 12.9 P2: classes, with/lookup, eval front end, Spike B, infra

**Result.** P2 merged into `m0/m2` in the plan order (ACCEPT-1 → CLS-VM → CLS-CG → EVAL-FE → LOOKUP → SPIKE-B → INFRA2, e293355). CLS-CG step 2 had merged CLS-VM into its own branch first (c584262) and ran the joint class checks there. The integration fixer (15 commits, 451c899) fixed one merge break: LOOKUP made an EVAL-FE test stale. Then 4 review-fix branches merged (431b48b, e97196d, 3ed2132, 3cca9ab), with 4 integration commits after them (e63f980, 49eccfc, ac9a5d8, 35d701d). All of this merged as P2-FIX (f0d9584). A second, smaller review round on the review fixes found 5 more defects; they were fixed on the same branch (c2de077, 88e7c26, ecde981) together with one x86 test fix (fcc74ae) and merged as P2-FIX round 2 (bf424c7).

### 12.10 P3 plan (plan v5) and the P2 -> P3 hand-off

Plan v5 re-checks P3 against the P2 head 5c9034a (tag `m2-p2`): 4 checkers (spikeb-bench, eval-fnctor, slots-isolate, adr-accept-cross) found 58 claims that were false or partly true (12 + 19 + 10 + 17), all applied; the editor took D1-D19 and the orchestrator O1-O5. The 15 DECIDED rows of the hand-off stay in force; where a plan-v5 decision differs, the row says which one wins. The ownership check gives 0 overlaps in 21 pairs between the 7 P3 roles. Merge order: ACCEPT-2 -> ADR-JIT -> FNCTOR -> ABI-SLOTS -> ISOLATE-API -> EVAL-VM -> SPIKEB-BENCH. P3 base: m0/m2 after the M2-AO fix (e719ffa) and this section.

### 12.11 P3: D21 bench, eval in the VM, Function(), object slots, isolate API, JIT ADR drafts

**Result.** P3 merged into `m0/m2` in the plan order (ACCEPT-2 → ADR-JIT → FNCTOR → ABI-SLOTS → ISOLATE-API → EVAL-VM → SPIKEB-BENCH, 6146ec5) with no conflict. EVAL-VM step 2 had merged FNCTOR into its own branch first (07695c8) and ran the joint eval and Function() checks there. The orchestrator then set the ADR status cells (1a0a33a). Three fixer branches merged (FIX-DOCS 5b310ce, FIX-D21 1e1c10f, FIX-EXP 89e11f7), then ADRLINE `--fix` (077d1fe, 20 refs). Review round 1 (Codex in 3 slices, the Claude reviewer on the hot path and on FIX-D21) gave 9 findings. Eight were fixed (3de84bc, 3b60b69, 4a65d74, 65f346b, dc1a071, 21c569b), and one needs ABI 5 (M2-P3-RF1-EVAL-PARAM). Round 2 (Codex on the review fixes, 37 KB) found nothing. Local CI found one break in a round-2 test (dac296e).

### 12.13 P4: Promise and builtins, placement (b), Win64 static unwind, B2/B3 records (as built), review and fixes

**Result.** P4 merged into `m0/m2` in this order: ACCEPT-3 (b0db0ec), PROMISE (b519743), B-OBJECT (3d28fc5), B-ARRAY (aca7b8b), B-STRING (b556da3), WIN-STATIC-1 (0c25006), PLACEMENT-B (575569f). B-ARRAY step 2 merged PROMISE into its own branch first (ff35c97, D13). Then the integration fixer (6be566e, 7 commits) and the expectations agent (d726d26, 2ac911b) merged. Review round 1 had Codex in 5 slices and the Claude reviewer on 2 slices. It gave 18 findings; 16 are different (Q1 = P1, Q3 = P2). Nine are fixed: A1, A2, S1 and Q1-Q3 in code (e7af527, 226a35f, 6e865f3, 78a4677), D1, H1 and H2 in the ADRs (23efac8, a5e0488). Six are category (c), and one is refuted. The 3 RF1 branches merged (7af018d, aea0cef, a027f72). The RF1 docs checker found 3 record problems, and a8eefa5 fixes them. Round 2: verdict approve, no findings (84 focused tests pass; the regression tests fail against d726d26).

Placement (b) is built for AArch64. On x86-64 it refuses (Refusal::Arch), and every (b) check prints SKIP. Its one provisional run is void; gate window 2 gives the verdict. The Win64 unwind test ran only as the static check on mac a64; its first real run is the CI windows-x64 step. B2 and H05 records: ACCEPT-3 wrote rows 2-4 of H01, H06, H09, H10, H11 and H28, and rows 1, 3 and 4 of H05. The RF1 docs fixer filled H05 row 2 from the m2p4-h05 and m2p4-docs Codex runs. B3 records (H04, H23, H24, H25, H26): ACCEPT-3 wrote rows 1 and 4; the RF1 docs fixer filled row 2 from the m2p4-docs Codex run (0 findings on B3); row 3 is P5-ACCEPT-4's.

**Result.** P5 merged into `m0/m2` in this order: B-REFLECT-GLOBALS (42fefe3), VM-SMALL steps 1 and 2 (c4b72d3), DOCS-SYNC (9981dca), ACCEPT-4 (ec9014c). VM-SMALL step 2 merged B-REFLECT-GLOBALS into its own branch first (77c6376, M2-P5-PLAN-D1). DOCS-SYNC is on VM-SMALL step 1 (bdd2009, P5-DS-1). Then one merge (b6109b3) brought in the branch m0m2/p5-fixer with 15 commits: the integration fixer (7 commits, 0728067 to 676dc5c), the expectations agent (6725cee), the round 2 docs fixer (9b2a520, 6684988, c4ca7de), the orchestrator B3 and H28 edits (d6c6063, f7187b9, 71ff088) and the H28 approval row of a second agent (e934f8a). Review round 1 had the Claude reviewer on the VM-SMALL tree (R1-R3) and Codex in 4 slices (X1, X2, D1, A1, A2). It gave 8 findings; 7 are different (X1 = R1). X2 was already fixed on the merged tree by VM-SMALL step 1. The other 6 are fixed: R1, R2 and R3 in code (48dd439, e4d58f4 and 676dc5c, 2bc74e8), D1, A1 and A2 in the docs (9b2a520, 6684988, c4ca7de). The first Codex run on the vmsmall slice was blocked by the content filter after it found R2 on its own; the second run gave approve. Round 2: the Claude fixer reviewer found no defect, and Codex on the fixer diff (ec9014c..676dc5c) gave approve, no findings.

H23, H24, H25 and H26 are Accepted (14 Accepted, 21 Draft, 2 Not started). ## 13. M0 month 3

### 13.1 Plan (plan v8)

**Result.** Plan v8 covers month 3 (M3-P0 to M3-P5 and W12, to the M0 exit). It was written on the final M2-P5 code tree b6109b3 by 3 section drafters, assembled, audited with 3 lenses (references, feasibility, completeness: 44 findings, 43 applied, 1 rejected) and checked with the owner checker (`check_m3.py`: 0 overlaps in every phase). The plan, the owner map and the checker stay outside the repo: scratchpad m0m3/plan-v8.md, m3-owners.json, check_m3.py; the audit record is scratchpad m0m3/plan-v8-changes.md. Phases: M3-P0 (P0-LANG4 ABI 5, P0-VM3 OBJECT_LAYOUT 3, P0-B-X64), M3-P1 (ASYNC, REALMS, B-DATE, B-COLLECTIONS, B-JSON, SP-UNIT, ACCEPT-5), M3-P2 (ASYNCGEN, VM-ARGS, MODULE, B-SMALL, PERF3, WIN-STATIC-2, ACCEPT-6), M3-P3 (FACTS-EXPORT, IMPORT-TLA stretch, EMBED-MIGRATE, FREEZE-PREP, ACCEPT-7, GATES-2), M3-P4 (ACCEPT-8, fixes, docs), M3-P5 (ACCEPT-9, GATES-3 = the M0 exit run). Accepted ADRs by phase: 14 -> 21 -> 24 -> 25 -> 26, then H20 Frozen. Every file:line below was read at b6109b3. Plan ids are in brackets.
